### 2026-09-27 — Closing a notification-opened case detail lands on the landing page, not a black screen — COMPLETE

- **The report:** the app is closed; a hearing notification is tapped and opens that case's detail view; closing the detail leaves the app on a black screen. It should return to the user's configured default landing page.
- **The root cause was the stack, not the close button.** A notification opens the detail as the stack's **only** route: `appRouterProvider` sets it as `initialLocation` on a cold launch, and `NotificationTapListener._openCase` uses `router.go(...)` on a warm tap (a `go` replaces the whole stack). The Close buttons and the delete flow called `Navigator.of(context).pop()` unconditionally, so at the root they emptied the navigator and GoRouter threw ("nothing to pop") leaving a blank surface. Popping was only ever correct for the normal path, where a case card `push`es the detail on top of its list.
- **The fix — one `_close()` helper on the screen.** It asks the navigator first: `canPop()` true pops exactly as before, and at the root it goes through the router to `AppRoutes.landingPageRoute(settingsViewModelProvider.defaultLandingPage)` — the screen the user chose in View Settings — so the app always lands somewhere real. Both Close controls (the normal header and the case-not-found body) and the post-delete pop now call it.
- **The back gesture gets the same treatment.** The Scaffold is wrapped in a `PopScope(canPop: false)` that delegates to `_close()`, the same pattern `AppShellRoot` uses for its exit confirmation, so the Android back gesture cannot blank the app either. Dialogs and sheets are unaffected — they sit on top of the screen's route.
- **`maybeOf`, not `of`.** `GoRouter.of(context)` asserts when no router is above the screen, which broke the plain-`Navigator` harness in `case_detail_test.dart`'s delete test; `GoRouter.maybeOf(context)?.go(...)` keeps that harness working while the app always has a router.
- **Verified as a real regression, not a coincidence.** The new test was run against the old unconditional pop first and failed with three exceptions from the emptied navigator, then passed once `_close()` was restored.
- **Tests:** `flutter analyze` clean; new test *"closing a notification-opened detail returns to the landing page"* in `test/core/notification_tap_test.dart` (now 4 tests there); full `flutter test` **740 passed, 2 skipped**.
- **Version `0.0.280`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`).
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth a device check: cold-launch from a hearing notification, then Close and the back gesture, and confirm both land on the configured default landing page.

### 2026-09-26 — Settings > About: the developer card stays centred in landscape — COMPLETE

- **The request (Settings > About only):** on some devices and in horizontal mode the **Designed & Developed by** section sits on the left instead of staying centred. Fix it.
- **The cause was the `Stack` in `_DeveloperCard`, not the label.** A `Stack` hands its non-positioned child *loose* width (`StackFit.loose`) and then aligns it with its own `alignment`, which defaults to `topStart`. The identity `Column` has no width of its own, so it shrink-wrapped to its widest line — the single-line court name, `District & Sessions Judge Court, Narayanganj.`, ~641 px wide on a 900 px screen — and the default alignment pinned that ~641 px block to the card's left edge. Every line inside it then centred on the *block*, not on the card, so all of them read ~96 px left of the card's centre. In portrait the court line wraps to the full content width, the block becomes as wide as the card, and the bug is invisible; that is why only landscape (and wide devices) exposed it. The floating label was never the problem: it is a `Positioned(left: 0, right: 0)` + `Center`, which always spans the card.
- **The fix is one wrapper.** The card's `Padding` now sits in a `SizedBox(width: double.infinity)`, so the identity block spans the card exactly as the mockup's block-level `flex flex-col items-center` does, and the existing `CrossAxisAlignment.center` (and the court line's `TextAlign.center`) centre on the card. `_AppTitleCard` was already correct for the same reason — it has no `Stack`, so the list's tight width reaches its `Column` — and the developer card now behaves identically. A comment on the wrapper records why.
- **Measured before and after with a probe test** (since deleted): at 900x420 the identity lines sat at `centerDx=353.6` against a card centre of 450; after the fix all of them, and the floating label, report `450`. Portrait and large-text results are unchanged (210 on a 420 px screen).
- **A regression test pins it.** `settings_about_test.dart` gained *developer card keeps its identity block on the card centre*: it mounts About on a 900x700 surface and asserts the label, the name and the role all share the surface centre line. On the 420x1400 harness the same assertion cannot fail, which is exactly why the old layout passed every existing test.
- **Tests:** `settings_about_test.dart` 12 passed (1 new); `flutter analyze` clean. No ARB, dependency or string changes.
- **Version `0.0.271`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`).
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth one device check: open Settings > About in landscape and confirm the developer card's photo, name, role and court line stay on the card's centre line.

### 2026-09-26 — The Edit Profile sheet no longer overflows above the keyboard — COMPLETE

- **The request (Settings > Account & Profile > Profile > Edit Profile form only):** tapping any text box raises the keyboard and the form reports *Bottom overflowed by 177 pixels*. Why, and fix it.
- **Why.** The form is a `showModalBottomSheet(isScrollControlled: true)` whose content is a plain `Column(mainAxisSize: min)` — a fixed-height block, not a scrollable one. It already lifts itself above the keyboard by adding `MediaQuery.viewInsets.bottom` to its bottom padding, but that padding only *moves* the form up; it does not let it get shorter. The sheet's maximum height is the screen, so once the keyboard covers the bottom the required height becomes `form + keyboard`, which is larger than the screen — and a `Column` that cannot shrink clips its last children and paints the overflow banner. The form is ~600 px tall and the keyboard is the rest, which is exactly where the 177 px comes from.
- **The fix is one wrapper.** The `Column` now sits inside a `SingleChildScrollView`, whose own size is the child's height clamped to the space available, so it scrolls instead of overflowing. On a normal screen nothing changes — the scroll view shrinks back to the content height, so the sheet is still only as tall as the form — and with the keyboard up the form is scrollable in the area above it. No widget was moved, restyled or re-ordered, and the existing keyboard padding was kept.
- **This is the pattern the repo already uses** for the same class of bug: the Language screen's body is a `SingleChildScrollView` and `language_screen_test.dart` pins that it "scrolls instead of overflowing on a short, large-text screen". The Profile form was the outlier that lacked it.
- **A regression test reproduces the exact bug.** `test/features/profile_name_test.dart` gained *the edit form scrolls clear of the keyboard*: it mounts the Profile screen on a 400x800 viewport, opens the sheet, sets a 300 px bottom `viewInsets` (the keyboard) and asserts no exception plus a scroll view inside the `BottomSheet`. **Verified non-vacuous:** before the fix it failed with `A RenderFlex overflowed by 186 pixels on the bottom` (the test viewport's version of the reported 177 px), and it passes after.
- **Tests:** the full suite is **738 passed, 2 skipped** (1 new); `flutter analyze` clean. No ARB or dependency changes.
- **Version `0.0.270`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`).
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth one device check: open Edit Profile and tap the last field (Mobile No) so the keyboard covers the Save button, then scroll to reach it.

- **The request (after the side menu's black selection):** audit the app for the remaining blue selected/active indicators and unify them on the same black as the side menu. Three choices were put to the user first: the scope of "blue", whether the already-dark active states should also move to pure black, and what replaces the dropdown's blue-tinted selected row.
- **The user chose the widest scope, so the change is a palette change, not thirty edits.** Every blue in the app was a token in `lib/core/constants/app_colors.dart`, so the tokens were repointed and one file repainted every accent: `secondary` (the action blue `#2563EB`) → black, `secondaryContainer` (`#316BF3`, the Case Detail Next Date card) → black, `secondaryHover` (the dropdown's selected-row tint) → a neutral 5% black, and the pale blue tints `secondaryFixed`, `secondaryFixedDim`, `tertiaryFixed`, `primaryFixed`/`primaryFixedDim` → neutral greys so the Case Reference chip and the More About cards stay legible. Their doc comments were rewritten to say so; the token names were kept so every call site compiles unchanged.
- **The black is `Colors.black`, by precedent.** `app_theme.dart` already paints Filled/Elevated/Outlined/Text buttons black-on-white, the side menu's active pill is `Colors.black`, and the Language picker's selected card has been `Colors.black` with a white check since it was built (pinned by `language_screen_test.dart`). The repointed tokens now carry `Color(0xFF000000)` so those accents land on exactly that black.

### 2026-09-26 — The bottom navigation changes screens with no motion at all — COMPLETE

- **The request (the screen change the bottom nav performs only):** the transition shown when the user changes screen from the bottom navigation was making some users feel unwell, so it must be removed — no transition at all while the screen changes. Nothing else in the app was to change.
- **The motion was GoRouter's default page transition, not anything the app wrote.** The bottom bar calls `context.go(route)`, and each tab's `GoRoute` was declared with a plain `builder:`, so GoRouter wrapped it in the platform's default page — on Android the zoom-and-fade that was the complaint. There was no `pageTransitionsTheme` and no custom page anywhere in the app, so there was nothing to tune: the eight tab routes are now declared with `pageBuilder:` returning a **`NoTransitionPage`**, which has no animation to run.
- **Scope is the eight screens a tab can open** — Daily Cause List, All Cases, Active, Upcoming, Overdue, Disposed, Notes and Case Reference — declared in `lib/core/router/app_router.dart`. Every other route (case detail, Settings and its children, login/signup/paywall/checkout) keeps the app's normal transition, because the `builder:` form was left untouched everywhere else.
- **One consequence, recorded rather than hidden:** GoRouter chooses the transition from the *route*, not from what was tapped, so those eight screens are also instant when the drawer opens one of them or when All Cases' summary cards push Active or Disposed. Narrowing that to the bottom nav alone would mean duplicating the routes or threading a transient "came from the bar" flag, which is far more machinery than the requirement is worth; the side effect is consistent (a case screen appearing instantly) rather than surprising.
- **A regression guard was added, because nothing else would notice.** `test/core/screen_transitions_test.dart` reads the router source and pins that every screen the bottom bar can open is declared with `NoTransitionPage` and is *not* back on the animated `builder:`. It takes the tab list from `bottomNavTabDefs`, so adding a tab fails the guard until its route is brought in line. **Verified non-vacuous:** reverting Notes to `builder:` was made to fail the guard, then restored. This follows the repo's existing source-guard pattern (`notification_local_only_test.dart`) for rules that no widget test can assert — a transition is motion, not behaviour.
- **Tests:** the full suite is **~731 passed, 2 skipped** (2 new in `screen_transitions_test.dart`); `flutter analyze` clean.
- **Version `0.0.267`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). No dependency changes and no UI text, so no ARB work.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth a device look at the three ways into these screens: a bottom-bar tap, a drawer item, and the All Cases summary card — all three should appear with no motion.

### 2026-09-26 — The Android launcher icon draws the logo large, not as a shrunken legacy icon — COMPLETE

- **The request (the app logo only):** the logo the Android app drawer shows is small — make it as big as it can be so the logo and the *Legal Case Diary* text are clearly seen, without the logo being cut.
- **The cause was the icon *type*, not the artwork.** With only a plain `ic_launcher.png`, Android 8+ gives the icon the **legacy treatment** — it shrinks the art onto a plate, which is the "small" the user saw. An **adaptive icon** is drawn at full size and masked by the launcher itself, so that is the fix; the artwork was not touched.
- **The spec, checked rather than recalled.** The adaptive canvas is **108 dp**, masked to at most **72 dp**, with a 66 dp recommended safe circle (Android docs). The logo is a page-filling "book cover" (its content spans the whole image vertically), so it is the **diagonal** that decides cropping: a centred logo whose diagonal stays under the 72 dp mask is never clipped.
- **The size was chosen with the numbers, not by eye.** `assets/icons/app_logo.png` is the foreground on a **white** plate (`#FFFFFF`), which the navy book sits on (its own white page edge blends into it). **Inset 22%** is set at the user's request, past the fully-safe size: the canvas is 108 dp and may be masked to 72 dp (36 dp radius), and at 22% the farthest logo pixel lies **37.58 dp** from the centre — **1.58 dp (~6 px at xxxhdpi) beyond the rim**, so the outermost corner of the cover's page edge is trimmed slightly. The steps above it: 23% is 36.23 dp (sub-pixel past the rim) and **24% is the largest inset at which *every* pixel stays inside (34.89 dp, 25% being 33.55 dp)** — the value to return to for zero trimming. The first pass at **12%** failed the "must not be cut" requirement outright: it drew the logo bigger (76% of the canvas) but put its farthest pixel at **51.0 dp**, so the cover's outer corners and its white page-edge strokes were clipped well inside the mask.
- **The background must be opaque — a transparent one renders as a black plate.** Asked for a transparent background, the next attempt set `adaptive_icon_background: "#00000000"`; installed on the device, the launcher drew a **black** area around the book, where the original plain-PNG icon (which has no background layer at all) had shown none. This is the adaptive-icon spec, not the phone: Android Developers' *Implementing Adaptive Icons* states the layers are 108 dp and that **"background drawables must be opaque whilst foregrounds can contain transparency"**, and `flutter_launcher_icons` carries it as a still-open request (#535, where `#00000000` is reported as not working). **A transparent adaptive-icon background is not something this format can carry**, so the only levers are the plate colour and the inset. The plate is **white** `#FFFFFF` (the alternative offered was the cover's own navy `#223B59`, which reads slightly larger because it merges with the cover; the user chose white). **Baking the logo into a plain PNG was considered and rejected:** the icon this task started from *was* a plain PNG, and a non-adaptive icon is exactly what makes the launcher wrap the artwork in its own background and scale it down to about two-thirds — the small icon the task set out to fix.
- **Generated by the project's own tool**, so it can be regenerated: `dart run flutter_launcher_icons` now writes `mipmap-anydpi-v26/ic_launcher.xml` (background → `@color/ic_launcher_background`, foreground → `@drawable/ic_launcher_foreground` with `android:inset="22%"`), `values/colors.xml` (`ic_launcher_background` = `#FFFFFF`, opaque), and `drawable-{m,h,xh,xxh,xxx}dpi/ic_launcher_foreground.png` at 108/162/216/324/432 px. The existing legacy `mipmap-*/ic_launcher.png` files came out **byte-identical** and are left for API < 26; `assets/icons/app_logo.png` itself is unchanged, so the About screen is unaffected.
- **Two out-of-scope files were touched and reverted.** The generator also rewrote `ios/Runner.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` and `web/manifest.json`; `git diff` showed **line-ending-only** changes, so both were restored, leaving the diff to the Android icon and `pubspec.yaml`. iOS and web keep their current icons.
- **`flutter analyze` clean** (no Dart changed). The adaptive foreground PNGs were verified to be the correct 108–432 px canvases.
- **Version `0.0.266`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). No dependency changes.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. **This one needs a real device:** only a launcher can show the masked result, so reinstall the app and look at the drawer in the phone's own shape (circle on Pixel, squircle on Samsung). 22% is set: the logo is as large as asked, at the cost of about 6 px of the cover's outermost page-edge corner at xxxhdpi; 24% is the value for zero trimming. The plate colour (`adaptive_icon_background`) is the only other lever, and it has to be a real colour.

### 2026-09-26 — Backup & Restore's headings become real sub-menu buttons, and the template teaches the format — COMPLETE

- **The request (Settings > Backup & Restore only):** (1) design the Backup & Restore, Automatic Backup and Excel Backup Format section headings just like the app's other sub-menu buttons — the Account & Profile rows being the cited example; (2) add court names, case types, steps and the like to the Excel **template**, plus a `Guide` sheet explaining how the user adds their own data; (3) do **not** make Backup Status a button — it stays always open, as before.
- **The headings now render through `SettingsMenuCard`,** the shared control every Settings sub-menu already uses, with an expand chevron standing in for that card's "open a screen" arrow: same icon, Title Case title, new subtitle, same 12 px radius and 44 px row. The accordion behaviour is unchanged — one section open, the closed ones not built, the reveal still animated. This departs from the mockup, which draws those three as plain `h2` headings, so it is recorded as a user-requested deviation in Task 15's file. Three subtitle keys were added to both ARBs ("Back Up Or Restore Your Diary", "Choose How Often Your Diary Backs Up", "See The Format And Download A Sample").
- **Backup Status left the `_Section` enum entirely.** It is a report, not a set of options, so it renders as the mockup's plain heading over the status card and can never collapse — the tests now assert that no toggle and no chevron exist for it. `_SectionHeading` (label-lg, `onSurfaceVariant`, tracking 0.7) is the old header style kept for that one heading.
- **Three decisions were asked before any code,** because the request left them open: the reference sheets hold **the diary's own live data plus one clearly-labelled sample row**; the `Guide` is **English**, like the fixed sheet names, so one workbook reads the same in either app language; and the reader **skips** the extra sheets silently. That last one is what makes the feature work at all — the user downloads the template, fills it in, and restores that very file.
- **Why the reference sheets had to be live, not illustrative.** `cases.court_id` and `cases.on_behalf_id` are required foreign keys holding raw UUIDs, and `case_category_id` a nullable one; `case_type` and `last_step` are text holding a **name**. So injecting a case by hand means quoting the user's own ids and names — only the live database has them. `Courts`, `Case Categories`, `Case Types`, `Steps` and `On Behalfs` therefore list the non-deleted rows, ordered by their court category, labelled with the same category name the app shows (the screen passes `courtCategoryName` in, since the repository cannot see the localizations). Each sheet opens with one `Example — Sample Row` so the shape is visible even on an empty diary.
- **The sample case row is now a believable case.** New `exampleValues` in `buildExcelTemplate` lets the caller override the column-name placeholder per table and column, and the repository fills `court_id`, `on_behalf_id`, `case_category_id`, `case_type` and `last_step` from the first live reference row — so the example points at data the diary really holds instead of a wall of column names. A table with no rows supplies nothing and keeps its placeholder.
- **The Guide says what the screen could not.** One sentence per row: blank `id` for a fresh UUID; `court_id`/`on_behalf_id`/`case_category_id` quote ids from the reference sheets while `case_type`/`last_step` quote a name; every required cell must be filled or the whole file is refused by sheet and row; `created_at`/`updated_at` are required and dates are **Unix epoch milliseconds** (1 January 2026 is 1767225600000); do not rename sheets, headers or reorder columns; the workbook is plaintext; and restoring replaces only the four data sheets. That last point answers the biggest practical friction in this format — nothing else tells the user what unit a date cell takes.
- **Only the template has the extra sheets.** A real Excel export still writes its four data sheets plus `Info`. The reader now accepts `excelTemplateSheets` and ignores them; a genuinely unknown sheet is still refused by name, which the format suite still pins. `buildExcelTemplateWorkbook` is now `async` (it queries the database), and a new `ExcelTemplateSheet` value type carries a scripted sheet — headers plus rows — so the Guide needs no special writer support.
- **A repo defect surfaced and was fixed, and it is worth knowing about.** `flutter gen-l10n` (needed for the new keys) revealed that the checked-in generated localizations had drifted from the ARBs: `settingsBackupRestore_restore` and `settingsBackupRestore_lastSuccessfulBackup` were still sentence case in the generated Dart although the ARBs had already been moved to Title Case in 0.0.263. The regeneration is correct — the ARB is the source of truth — so the screen test's two stale expectations were updated to "Restore from Backup" and "Last Successful Backup". **Any future `flutter gen-l10n` run will make this change again,** which is why it was fixed rather than hand-patched around.
- **Tests (2 new in `test/core/excel_backup_test.dart`, 2 new in `backup_repository_test.dart`, the screen suite updated; suite 728 passed, 2 skipped, `flutter analyze` clean).** The format suite writes extra sheets and proves the reader still reads only the four data tables while the `exampleValues` override wins. The repository suite proves the **live** court id, name and category label really reach the Courts sheet and that the sample restores with its case quoting the diary's own court and role. The screen suite now pins that the three headings are `SettingsMenuCard`s and that Backup Status has neither toggle nor chevron.
- **Version `0.0.264`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). No dependency changes.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. **Two things deserve a real-device look:** the hand-built workbook must still open in Excel with its new sheets (only Excel can prove that), and the three new heading buttons should be checked against the Account & Profile rows on a phone, since their `Card` carries a 4 px default margin that the surrounding cards here do not.

### 2026-09-26 — Backup & Restore gains an Excel file, and its sections fold away — COMPLETE

- **The request (Settings > Backup & Restore only):** make Backup & Restore and Automatic Backup collapsible, one open at a time; add a **Backup as Excel File** button after Backup to Local Storage; let Restore take an Excel file back; and add a section after the buttons explaining the format, with a sample template.
- **Two plan rules could not both hold, so I asked first.** The plan's backup is a versioned JSON envelope and §12.3.1 says never put a plaintext export in shared storage — but a spreadsheet the user opens in Excel **cannot** carry the app's authenticated container. You chose plaintext with a warning; it is recorded as a deliberate deviation in `docs/tasks/15_backup_restore_and_free_tier_gating.md`, and the encrypted `.lcbak` stays the canonical, complete backup.
- **The chosen dependency turned out to be impossible.** `excel` pins `xml <7`, while Task 14's `pdf ^3.13.0` needs `xml ^7.0.1` — they can never resolve together. You picked the alternative: the container is **hand-built OOXML** on `archive` + `xml`, both already in the lock file at exactly the versions this needs and now promoted to direct dependencies, so no other feature moved and no third-party spreadsheet code entered the app. Writing is string-built; reading also understands Excel's shared-string table, so a workbook the user opened and re-saved still restores.
- **The four sheets are the four tables a lawyer edits** — `cases`, `case_history`, `notes`, `case_references` — plus an `Info` sheet. **Columns are read from the live Drift schema** (`$columns` / `requiredDuringInsert`), so the headers, the downloadable template and the reader cannot drift: adding a column to a table adds it to all three. Sheet names stay fixed English identifiers so one file works in either app language.
- **The dangerous part of a partial file was the restore, not the export.** `restoreSnapshot` used to wipe **all twenty** portable tables and insert whatever the file held — so a four-sheet workbook would have deleted courts, steps, preferences and the queue, leaving cases pointing at nothing. The collision check and the delete-all now walk **the tables the file carries**, in reverse FK order. A JSON backup carries all twenty, so its behaviour is exactly as before; a workbook replaces only its four and leaves the rest alone. `hasDataInTables` runs the same scoping for the pre-replace question, so a workbook never asks about data it would not touch.
- **Rules that make a hand-edited workbook safe.** A blank `id` gets a fresh UUID — that is what makes a row typed into Excel importable — while any other blank in a NOT NULL column is refused **naming the sheet and the spreadsheet row** before any SQL runs. Rows otherwise import verbatim with their ids and all five sync columns, so a restored row can be pushed unchanged later. An unknown or renamed sheet or column is refused by name too, which is exactly the "organize the file in the specified format" the explainer promises.
- **Restore is one flow for both formats.** An Excel file is recognised by its ZIP signature **before** the passphrase window, which it would otherwise be asked for and could never satisfy; from there both formats become an `OpenedBackup` and share the confirm step, the transaction and the reload. The replace window says *Replace the data in these sheets?* and lists them for a workbook, instead of the *everything on this device* warning that is only true of a full backup.
- **The screen.** All four sections are one-open-at-a-time accordions (Backup & Restore open first; tapping the open heading shuts it), a closed section is not built at all, and the reveal animates so the list does not jump under the user's thumb. **Backup as Excel File** sits second, directly after the local backup, with the plaintext warning added to the note above it. The new **Excel Backup Format** section states the requirement — sheet names, header row, blank-id rule — admits the file is not a complete backup, and offers **Download Template File**, generated from the same column list the reader validates.
- **Tests (16 in `test/core/excel_backup_test.dart`, 5 added to `backup_repository_test.dart`, 6 added/rewritten in `backup_restore_screen_test.dart`; suite 725 passed, 2 skipped).** The format suite round-trips every value kind, proves the reader handles an **Excel-shaped** workbook it did not write itself, and pins each refusal to its sheet, column and spreadsheet row. The repository suite proves the sheet columns equal the live schema, that a workbook import replaces only its own tables (the court outside them survives), and that a blank id becomes a real one. The screen suite drives the real engine end to end: the Excel export, the template, a workbook restore that skips the passphrase, and an off-format workbook whose message names the header to fix.
- **The one behavioural change to an existing test, called out:** *restoring over existing data asks first* seeded its "existing data" in `sync_metadata`, which a workbook does not carry — so with scoped replace it correctly stops asking. It now seeds a note (a table the file does carry) and asserts the `sync_metadata` row **survives** the replace, which is the new behaviour made explicit rather than papered over.
- **Version `0.0.263`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). Two direct dependencies promoted (`archive`, `xml`); nothing new added. Task 15's file carries the deviation section for Part B.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. **This feature needs a real-device check more than most:** only Excel itself can prove the hand-built workbook opens, so save one, open it in Excel, edit a cell, re-save, and restore that file.

### 2026-09-26 — A folded court opens again the next day — COMPLETE

- **The request (Daily Cause List to begin with, then agreed for all three grouped lists):** the screen lets the user hide a court's cases by tapping its heading, and the app kept that choice indefinitely; it should reset every day, so the first time the user opens it after midnight the list starts with every court showing.
- **The fold was already per screen and persistent — only its lifetime changed.** `caseTableCollapsedCourts` (screen id → folded court ids) is untouched; a new sibling map `caseTableCollapsedDays` (screen id → Bangladesh start-of-day epoch-ms) records *when* each screen's folds were made. Same `view_settings_json` blob, same tolerant parsing, no schema change and no new column.
- **The rule lives in the two shared helpers**, so all three grouped lists get it and there is nothing to keep in step per screen: `watchCaseTableCollapsedCourts` returns nothing when a screen's stamp is not today, and `setCaseTableCourtCollapsed` starts from today's folds only and stamps the set it writes with today.
- **Reading never writes.** A stale entry is simply ignored until the user folds something, so no screen writes during a build; and because the write starts from today's folds, the new day's first fold **replaces** yesterday's set instead of reviving it (which is what a naive "clear on read" would have done on the second day). A missing stamp counts as an earlier day, so an install upgrading from a build without stamps opens fully expanded.
- **The stamp is per screen, not global.** One app-wide day would have been wrong: folding a court on the Daily Cause List today would have refreshed Overdue Dates' stale folds too. The comparison is `AppDateUtils.bangladeshTodayStartOfDay()`, the app's civil day everywhere, so the rollover is Bangladesh midnight.
- **Tests (3 new in `case_list_view_mode_test.dart`, 1 new round-trip in `repositories_test.dart`; suite 696 passed, 2 skipped).** Yesterday's fold opens expanded; today's first fold drops yesterday's and is stamped today — the test that would catch a stale set being carried forward; and one screen's new day leaves another screen's fold alone. The first draft returned a `const` empty set from the shared helper, so the first tap threw `Unsupported operation`; the three existing fold tests caught it at once, and they were left unchanged because the same-day behaviour must not move.
- **Version `0.0.262`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). Task 09's refinement log carries the note.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth a device check at the rollover itself: fold a court, leave the app, move the phone's date past Bangladesh midnight, and confirm the list opens with every court showing.

### 2026-09-26 — The checklist puts an item back where it was — COMPLETE

- **The request (the to-do editor only):** the Add Item button's text should be white, and unticking an item should return it to its original position instead of leaving it at the bottom.
- **The white was an override, not a missing style.** The app's global `textButtonTheme` already fills a TextButton black with white text; the Add Item label was overriding that foreground with `onSurfaceVariant`, which is what made it read grey. It and its `+` icon are now `AppColors.onPrimary` — the icon too, because a grey glyph beside white text is the same defect in a different place.
- **The position is now remembered per row.** Each row carries a fixed `order` — its place among the unticked rows, assigned when it is loaded or added — and ticking still sends a row to the very bottom. Unticking reinserts it **before the first unticked row that outranks it**, so it lands exactly where it started rather than at the end of the unticked block (which is what a plain stable partition gave). A row added later takes the next `order`, so it stays at the end of the unticked block.
- **Tests (1 rewritten, 1 new; suite 692 passed, 2 skipped).** The unticking test now seeds three rows and checks the full round trip — middle row to the bottom when ticked, back between its neighbours when unticked, with the strike-through cleared. The new one pins the Add Item label at `AppColors.onPrimary`, which is the only thing that would notice the colour being overridden again.
- **Version `0.0.261`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`).
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16.

### 2026-09-26 — A note can be a checklist now — COMPLETE

- **The request (Notes screen only):** tapping the + button should offer two kinds — text note and to-do; a text note stays as it is, and a to-do's screen is the same except the body becomes Google Keep's list.
- **The kind is the data, not a flag.** `notes.items` (nullable, JSON `[{text, done}]`) holds the checklist and `null` means a text note, so nothing has to keep a second field in step — and an empty to-do, stored as `[]`, stays a to-do. Schema 8→9 with an `addColumn` migration, Drift regenerated, and `items` added to the repository's sync payload/changed fields.
- **`body` mirrors the item texts**, one per line, which is the whole reason the plan §12.1 title+body search finds a to-do by what is on its list with **no query change at all** (the user's choice on search). The checklist itself always renders from `items`.
- **The editor is the same screen.** Only the body differs: for a to-do it is a row of tick + editable item + remove, with an Add Item row beneath, and Enter on a row opens the next — Keep's own flow. Ticking drops the row below the unticked ones and strikes it through, applied as a **stable partition** (unticked first, each block keeping its order), so unticking lifts it back without shuffling anything else. A new to-do opens with one empty row; blank rows are dropped on save.
- **The + button asks first.** A small bottom sheet with `Text Note` and `To-Do`, matching the app's other choosers; dismissing it creates nothing, and Text Note is exactly the old flow. A to-do's card shows its first three rows with their ticks (struck through when done) and `+N More`, so the two kinds are told apart at a glance.
- **Flagged, not silent:** the plan's `notes` schema (§ `notes`) lists `title` + `body` only, so this is a deliberate, user-requested extension of the source of truth — recorded in `docs/tasks/11_notes_and_case_reference.md` with the note that **Part B needs `items` on the server table**. The plan itself was not edited.
- **Tests (13 new in `test/features/notes_todo_test.dart`; suite 691 passed, 2 skipped).** The codec (round-trip, empty-is-still-a-to-do, a mistyped row degrading, the body mirror), the editor (tick reorder with strike-through, untick rejoining the unticked block, and a real save that drops a blank row and writes both `items` and the mirror), the screen (the two options, To-Do opening the checklist, a card showing three rows and counting the rest), and two search/sync guards — one of which is the only thing that would notice the body mirror going missing.
- **Version `0.0.260`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`).
- **A stray test host, cleaned up:** an earlier run of this suite was killed mid-flight and left `flutter_tester.exe` holding `build/native_assets/windows/sqlite3mc.dll`, which made the next `flutter test` crash on startup with `PathAccessException`; the process was terminated and the directory removed. The suite now takes its widget trees down inside the test body, before the database closes.

### 2026-09-26 — A case can pick its own Notification Time — COMPLETE

- **The request (Add/Edit Case form and Quick Update window only):** put the Day Offset on the left half of its line with a Notification Time on the right; the time button must open the same window Settings → Notifications uses; a case follows the Settings time until the user picks one, and then its own time overrides the default.
- **The override is a real column, not a JSON hack.** `case_notification_preferences` gained nullable `time_minutes` (per-user preference class — no sync columns), schema 7→8 with an `addColumn` migration, Drift regenerated. Null is the honest "follows the default": nothing writes a copied default, so a later change in Settings moves every case that never picked a time.
- **One window, one file.** The Settings screen's time picker (the Bengali 12-hour override plus the app's 112x48 dialog actions) moved into `notification_time_picker.dart` as `showNotificationTimePicker(context, initialMinutes)`; Settings now calls it too, so the two surfaces cannot drift.
- **The delivery had to change to honour it.** The app armed one daily alarm at the Settings time and posted every due alert then, which makes a per-case time meaningless. It now arms **one alarm per distinct time in use** (the Settings time plus each case's own), the alarm id encodes its minutes (`alertAlarmIdBase + minutes`), and the job reads its slot from the id it is handed — so a slot posts only the cases whose effective time is that slot, and each slot re-arms its own daily chain. Ids are kept clear of the old single alarm's `1301`, so an alarm left over from an earlier build is treated as unknown (it posts what is due, then hands over) instead of being misread as midnight. `syncAlertAlarms` remembers the armed times in SharedPreferences, so a slot the settings no longer want is cancelled rather than left to wake the phone once more, and switching alerts off cancels them all. Re-armed on settings writes and now on case save/quick update.
- **UI:** the Schedule card's Day Offset row is now `[Day Offset | Notification Time]`, matching the form's labelled fields; the time field shows the effective time, opens the shared window, shows a `schedule` icon while it follows the default and a restore button (tooltip reuses `settingsViewSettings_fontSizeReset`) once it has its own — the only way back to the default.
- **Tests (4 new in `notification_plan_test.dart`, 2 new in `task07_acceptance_test.dart`, 1 new in `schema_test.dart`, 1 extended in `repositories_test.dart`; suite 678 passed, 2 skipped).** The planner tests pin `effectiveTimeMinutes` (including a corrupt value), `distinctNotificationTimes` (dedup, sort, drop nonsense), that a slot run posts only its own cases, and that no slot means "post everything" as before. The widget pair drives the real window through its keyboard entry — the form shows `09:30 AM` from Settings, overrides to `11:05 AM` and resets; Quick Update stores `timeMinutes` for one case. The source guard in `notification_local_only_test.dart` now pins the new entry-point signature and the id-to-slot line.
- **Version `0.0.259`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`).
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth a device pass on the new scheduling: give one case a time an hour out and confirm only that case alerts then, with the others still arriving at the Settings time.

### 2026-09-23 — The Month column centres, the Year sits a seam gap past the middle, and the columns click as they scroll — COMPLETE

- **The request (the same Month/Year selector):** increase the distance of Month and Year by 50%; make the Month texts middle aligned; and give the columns a light scroll sound as the user scrolls through them.
- **The distance is the Year column's lead-in, and it went 16 → 24 px** (`monthYearPickerSeamGap` is now `AppSpacing.lg`, a token rather than a bare 1.5×). It stays the only magic-free way to state a gap: the labels' own widths never move the pair.
- **The Month column centres its labels in its own half** (it was right-aligned against the seam — the user's choice between the three readings on offer), so the 12 month names no longer hang off the middle at different distances, and the Year column starts 24 px into the other half.
- **A light platform click per row that passes the middle.** `SystemSound.play(SystemSoundType.click)` fires from the columns' `onSelectedItemChanged`, which is the callback that already knows a row has settled into the middle — no new listener, no timer. It is deliberately the **platform** sound, not a bundled asset: Android plays its own touch-sound click and the phone's "Touch Sounds" setting decides whether it is heard at all, so the app can never be noisier than the user's own phone. Nothing plays when the picker opens (the callback only reports a *change*). Worth knowing: on iOS the framework's picker-specific `SystemSoundType.tick` is the sound iOS uses for exactly this, while `click` is its input click — the single cross-platform call was kept rather than branching per platform.
- **Tests (2 rewritten/new in `month_year_picker_test.dart`, 6 there; suite 671 passed, 2 skipped).** The alignment test now measures both halves: the Month label's centre is the left half's centre and the Year label's left edge is exactly one exported seam gap past the picker's middle. The new sound test mocks `SystemChannels.platform`, asserts the picker is silent as it opens, then that `jumpToItem` one row plays exactly one `SystemSoundType.click` — the first guard in this file for a platform call. `flutter analyze` clean.
- **Version `0.0.258`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). No new dependency, no asset.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth a device check with the phone's Touch Sounds both on and off, since the second case is silent by design and the first is the only way to hear what the click actually sounds like.

### 2026-09-23 — The Month and Year sit together in the middle, and the current value is a size bigger — COMPLETE

- **The request (the same Month/Year selector):** bring Month and Year a bit closer, and increase the selected text size a bit more.
- **Closer, without shrinking the columns.** Each column still gets half the dialog, but it now *holds its labels against the seam it shares with the other column* — the Month column right-aligned, the Year column left-aligned, each one `AppSpacing.md` (16 px) from the middle — so the pair reads as one "August 2026" instead of two lists at opposite sides of the width. Narrowing the columns instead was rejected: a wide Bengali month name (`সেপ্টেম্বর`) at the largest Font Size step on the smallest phone would have had almost no room left before the wheel's clip, and half-width columns keep that headroom whatever the label says.

Both columns keep their own current row, so either one reads as the live value at a glance.

The text test now asserts the size *relationship* (bigger than its neighbour) as well as bold and the app's text black, so a token change that flattened the step would fail it. The straight-list geometry test is unaffected and still measures rows one row extent apart with an unbent box. `flutter analyze` clean.

- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth a device look at the seam in বাংলা and at the largest Font Size step, since `সেপ্টেম্বর` is the widest label the Month column ever holds.

### 2026-09-23 — The calendar's month and year are a straight list, not a wheel — COMPLETE

- **The request (the calendars only: Add/Edit Case, Update, Daily Cause List):** stop bending the text of the scroll wheel — show a straight list; make the text black; show the text in the middle instead of under the line drawn there; and bring the whole thing onto the app's own theme. This supersedes the entry below (the wheels are gone).
- **Two of the four complaints were one defect.** `CupertinoPickerDefaultSelectionOverlay` is painted **on top of** the rows, and it was filled with an opaque `AppColors.surfaceDim` — the SDK's own doc says that colour must not be fully opaque "as the currently selected item of the underlying CupertinoPicker should remain visible". So the band was drawn over the selected Month/Year (the "horizontal line in the middle" hiding its text), and `CupertinoPicker` hardcodes `overAndUnderCenterOpacity: 0.447`, which is what made every other row read grey rather than black. Neither is settable on `CupertinoPicker`; the third complaint (the bend) is the cylinder transform the widget exposes no way to switch off.
- **So the picker is Flutter's own scroll view with the cylinder flattened.** New `lib/shared/widgets/month_year_picker.dart` replaces the two identical private copies (`_MonthYearWheels` + `_PickerWheel` lived in *both* dialogs), and each column is now a `ListWheelScrollView.useDelegate` — the widget that already provides the snapping and the `FixedExtentScrollController` — with `diameterRatio: 100`, `squeeze: 1`, `useMagnifier: false` and `overAndUnderCenterOpacity: 1`. Flutter has no flat build of this picker, so at that diameter the cylinder is flattened rather than the widget replaced: rows land exactly one row extent (44 px) apart and the outermost row tilts about half a degree.
- **Nothing is painted over the middle row at all.** Marking the current value is bold text alone (the user's choice) — no band and no rule — so the selected Month and Year are plain black on the dialog's own white, one weight heavier than their neighbours.
- **Theme alignment:** labels use the theme's own `bodyLarge` at `AppColors.onSurface` (the app text black, at full strength); the plain picker's Cancel/OK dropped raw `Colors.black`/`Colors.white` for `AppColors.primary`/`onPrimary`, the pair the Daily Cause List Close button already uses; and the `package:flutter/cupertino.dart` import is gone from both dialogs (the selection-overlay workaround went with it).
- **Behaviour deliberately unchanged:** both columns open from either header control, each on the focused month and year; spinning one moves only that column; tapping a row confirms the pair and returns to the day grid; 1900–2100 is still both the list and the bounds.
- **One accessibility change worth knowing:** `CupertinoPicker` wrapped the wheel in a private adjustable-semantics node (one node reading the current value, changed by swiping). A straight list has no such wrapper, so each row now exposes its own text and tap — a screen reader can step through the values and double-tap the one it wants, which is the same pattern the app's other pickers use.
- **Tests (4 new in `test/shared/widgets/month_year_picker_test.dart`; suite 669 passed, 2 skipped).** The geometry test *measures* straightness rather than asserting it: neighbouring rows are exactly one row extent apart (the wheel's 39 px squeeze would fail it) and a row's painted box equals its laid-out box (a tilted or magnified row paints a larger one). The others pin the flattening config, that every label is `AppColors.onSurface` with only the current row bold, and that a tap carries both columns' live positions. The two dialog test files moved from `CupertinoPicker` to `ListWheelScrollView` and to the exported column keys (`month-picker-column` / `year-picker-column`, replacing `picker-wheel-*`). `flutter analyze` clean.
- **Not covered by a mockup**, as before: no mockup defines this control.
- **Version `0.0.256`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). No new dependency.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth a device pass on the touch feel: one tap must still mean one confirmed choice, and the drag now has to feel like a list rather than a wheel.

### 2026-09-23 — The calendar's month and year are iOS wheels now — COMPLETE

- **The request (the calendars only: Add/Edit Case, Update, Daily Cause List):** the Month/Year selector should read the iOS way. This replaces the single-column list shape built earlier the same day (the entry below keeps that history; the lists are gone).
- **Both columns, side by side — the user's choice.** Tapping the Month *or* the Year in the header now opens one view holding two wheels (Month | Year), opened on the focused month and year, rather than a separate list per selector. Spinning a column moves only that wheel; **tapping a row confirms the pair** and returns to the day grid, which is the gesture the lists used before them.
- **Flutter's own wheel, not a hand-rolled one.** `CupertinoPicker` (core Flutter — no dependency, no pixel work) with `useMagnifier: true, magnification: 1.1`, which is what draws the centred row larger and fades the rows above and below: the iOS enlargement the earlier requirement asked for, without a hand-set font size. Its row height is 44 px, chosen so a magnified label still fits at the largest Font Size step, and the two columns join into one selection band (each drops the cap on the shared side).
- **Hard rule 7 was checked, not assumed.** The SDK source was read first: this version's selection overlay (`CupertinoPickerDefaultSelectionOverlay`) is a solid rounded rectangle — no `BackdropFilter`, which the project forbids. Its band is filled with the app's `AppColors.surfaceDim` so the wheel belongs to the palette, and each row carries the app's own `bodyLarge` colour instead of Cupertino's fallback style.
- **Tap-versus-spin, deliberately.** The framework's wheel also handles taps (it spins to the tapped row), so each row carries its own `GestureDetector`, which wins the gesture arena and turns one tap into exactly one confirmed choice rather than an animation.
- **Tests (2 rewritten, 2 re-scoped; suite 665 passed, 2 skipped).** The new pair asserts both columns appear from either header control, that each opens on the focused month/year, that the year column holds 201 rows (1900–2100), that spinning the year column alone moves nothing else, and that tapping the month row then applies *both* columns (the day grid returns, header reading the spun-to year). The two Bengali-digit tests now scan the year wheel by key. `flutter analyze` clean.
- **Version `0.0.255`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). No new dependency.
- **Not covered by a mockup**, as before: no mockup defines a month/year control. Worth a device pass — the wheel's haptics and its finger-tracking are the parts a widget test cannot exercise.
- **A formatting detour, reverted.** Running `dart format` over these four files (the SDK's new "tall style" in Dart 3.12) reflowed ~350 lines of code this change never touched — nothing in the repo is tall-style formatted. Its output was restored line for line to the previous arrangement, so the diff holds only this change; do not run `dart format` here expecting a no-op.

### 2026-09-23 — The Battery & Auto-Start Setup guide shows completed steps in green — COMPLETE

- **The request (Settings > Notifications only):** the guide's steps should show as completed, in green, from what the phone says rather than from what the user claims.
- **Two of the three steps are real OS checks.** Exact alarms were already read (`AndroidScheduler.canScheduleExactAlarms`), and battery optimization now is too: `PowerManager.isIgnoringBatteryOptimizations(packageName)` is the OS's own answer to that step — the exact state the phone's Battery-usage screen writes — and no installed plugin exposes it (`flutter_local_notifications` 22 has no battery API), so `MainActivity.kt` serves it over the app's own `com.legalcasediary.app/device_setup` method channel. The gateway stays the only file that touches platform code; a missing channel (the test host, the Windows build) answers `false`, which leaves the step un-ticked rather than falsely green.
- **Auto-start cannot be read at all** — it is an OEM feature, not an Android permission, so no public API reports it. On the user's decision that step is now theirs to tick: a `Mark As Done` / `Mark As Not Done` button on that one card, stored per user as `UserSettings.notificationAutoStartDone` in the same `view_settings_json` blob as the guide's other two flags (no schema change, and it travels with backup/restore).
- **A done step is green, and the check is live.** Badge becomes a green tick, the title turns `AppColors.success`, and the green line that replaces the button names how that step's completion is known (`Battery Usage Is Already Unrestricted`, `Exact Alarms Are Already Allowed`, `Auto-Start Marked As Done By You`) — so a tick always says what it is based on. The screen is now a `WidgetsBindingObserver` and re-reads both OS checks on every resume, because `Take Me There` hands the user to the phone and the new state is only knowable on the way back. A done step hides its `Take Me There` button, matching what the exact-alarm step already did.
- **Robustness:** the settings read that supplies the saved auto-start tick is guarded — this is the app's first screen on a fresh install, and a failed read must leave the steps usable (auto-start simply un-ticked) rather than hang the spinner.
- **Tests (6 new in `battery_setup_guide_test.dart`, 17 there; 1 new source guard).** Battery exempt → green title (asserted by rendered colour, not just copy), tick icon, no button; an outstanding step is not green; a step turns green after an `inactive` → `resumed` lifecycle round trip; the user's tick is stored, cleared, and still green when the guide is reopened; the completed state is localized in বাংলা. `notification_local_only_test.dart` gained a guard that the channel name and method name still match on both the Dart and Kotlin sides — a rename there would silently un-tick the step with nothing else to catch it.
- **Version `0.0.253`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). No new dependency, no mockup (this screen has none).
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth a device pass on one OEM phone: the battery step is the first thing in the guide that reads a real OS state, and the resume re-check is a platform behaviour the test host cannot show.

### 2026-09-22 — Hearing alerts show four lines, and the expanded view no longer cuts them — COMPLETE

- **The report (notifications only):** the expanded alert showed the case line and a `Petitioner vs Respondent` line that was cut off after a few characters. The wanted body is case, parties in full, court, step — the user chose to keep the court as line 3 and put the labelled step beneath it.
- **The truncation was not ours.** Neither the planner nor the form or DAO shortens a party name, and `flutter_local_notifications` only applies a style when the app passes one — so the alert went out with **no style at all** and Android laid the body out itself, ellipsising a line even when expanded. `_details()` now hands the body over as `BigTextStyleInformation`, which is the framework's own way of asking for the whole text: every body line on its own line, wrapped where long. The **test notification** shares that same call, so it is styled the same way.
- **The step is the fourth line, labelled.** `HearingCandidate` gained `lastStep`, filled from `cases.last_step` in the alarm job, resolved through `AppTranslations.stepNames` **with the case's own court category** — the same path the case card uses, because identical step names are labelled differently by different categories — and prefixed with the existing `caseCard_lastStep` label (`Step:` / `পদক্ষেপ:`). A case with no step keeps its three lines; an empty part is dropped rather than left as a blank line.
- **Tests (3 new in `notification_plan_test.dart`, 25 there; 2 guards added).** The body is asserted as the exact ordered list of four lines, a step-less case as three, the Bengali step resolved through the shared-category seed map *and* the by-token fallback, and a user-typed step kept verbatim. `notification_local_only_test.dart` — the file for rules that cannot run on a test host — gained two source-level guards: the gateway must post the body with a BigText style, and the job must pass `row.lastStep` into the candidate, since a silent drop there would lose the line with nothing to catch it.
- **Version `0.0.251`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). No new dependency.

### 2026-09-22 — Settings > View Settings gains an app-wide Font Size step — COMPLETE

- **The request:** a new Font Size section in Settings > View Settings, letting the user increase or decrease every text in the app by one font size at a time, relative to its current size.
- **One seam, not hundreds of call sites.** `TextScaler` is Flutter's own hook for this, so new `SteppedTextScaler` (`core/theme/font_size_scope.dart`) adds a whole number of logical pixels to every size and `FontSizeScope` installs it in `MaterialApp.builder` — one wrap in `app.dart` covers every route, dialog, bottom sheet, overlay, the drawer and the bottom bar, including text the app never styled. It is **additive, not multiplicative**: a 12 px label gains 1 px and a 30 px headline gains 1 px, where a scale factor would have grown the headline far more.
- **The device's own scaling is wrapped, never replaced** — the step is added on top of the platform's scaler, so this setting can never switch Android's accessibility text scaling off. At step 0 the scope adds no layer at all, so the widget tree is byte-for-byte what it was before this existed.
- **Stored where the other view settings live.** `UserSettings.fontSizeStep` rides the existing `user_preferences.view_settings_json` blob under `font_size_step` (no schema change, no migration) and is clamped to `fontSizeStepMin`..`fontSizeStepMax` (−2..+6) **on read as well as on write**, so an older build, a restored backup or a hand-edited value can never push text past what the layouts were measured for. 0 is the shipped default, so every existing install is unchanged until the user touches it.
- **The control is a − / value / + stepper.** The middle reads `Default` at 0 and `+2` / `-2` away from it in the language's own digits (`২`, `+৩`), tapping it resets to Default — the quick way back from the largest sizes — and a button at either end of the range is disabled rather than silently doing nothing. Its 48 px buttons are drawn in the picker row's own language.
- **Two things worth knowing.** The step does **not** touch printed output: the `printing` package builds its tree under its own `MediaQuery`, so a PDF keeps the layout its templates define. And the app's own body size (16 px) is what the framework's deprecated ratio getter is estimated against, since an additive step has no single ratio; nothing in the app reads it.
- **Recorded deviation:** plan §10.1 lists five View Settings keys and does not include this one, and this screen has no mockup, so the addition is a deliberate extension of the plan (user-requested) rather than a silent one. Worth a device pass at +6 px: the compact case cards and the fixed 48 px picker rows are the tightest layouts in the app, and `case_table_view` already asks the scaler for its row height, so the extended table grows with the step.
- **Tests (15 new; suite 648 passed, 2 skipped).** `test/core/font_size_scope_test.dart` (9) covers the additive maths at three sizes, negative steps, wrapping the platform scaler, equality, the deprecated-ratio estimate, that step 0 leaves the tree untouched, that a step really changes the size of the *painted* text (measured both up and down), and that the app root hands the saved step to the scope; `view_settings_test.dart` (6 more, 24 total) covers storing up and down, both ends of the range, the tap-to-reset, Bengali digits, and the read-side clamp. The nine existing dropdown tests now scroll their row into view before tapping — the added card pushed rows below the fold, where a tap silently opened nothing.
- **Version `0.0.250`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). No new dependency.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16.

### 2026-09-22 — The Profile screen starts blank for every user — COMPLETE

- **The report:** Settings > Account & Profile > Profile shipped the developer's own details, so every other install showed them; each user should instead see an empty profile and fill in their own.
- **The cause was seeded sample data, in one place.** `profile_screen.dart` created its controllers with the mockup's sample values (`Asadusjaman`, `Dhaka Bar Association`, `1557`, `Chamber 402 …`, `1812332721`) and `profile_name_store.dart` defaulted the full name to `defaultProfileFullName = 'Asadusjaman'`. Only the name was ever persisted, so even a user who typed their details lost them at the next launch and got the sample data back.
- **The record is now the whole form, blank by default.** `profile_name_store.dart` holds a `ProfileFields` record (five fields, `toJson`/`fromJson`/`trimmed`) under one `profile_fields` key and writes all five together, so what the user types survives restarts instead of reverting. `defaultProfileFullName` is gone; a new install reads `ProfileFields.blank`. `profileNameProvider` is now a derived `Provider<String>` over the record, so the eight read-only consumers (drawer header, the six case screens' client messages, the printed sheet) keep working unchanged while `setName` disappeared with its only caller.
- **Migration, so no saved name is lost:** when the record key is absent the store falls back to the legacy `profile_full_name` key (installs before this change), and the first save folds it into the record and removes it. A corrupt record falls back the same way rather than breaking the screen.
- **The screen reads the persisted record, not its controllers.** The read view renders `ref.watch(profileFieldsProvider)` (five `—` rows and the `Your Name` heading when blank — the empty state already existed, it was just unreachable) and the editor is re-seeded from the record each time it opens, so a sheet dismissed without saving leaves nothing half-typed behind. Save writes all five fields and the toast follows.
- **Mockup tension, settled by the earlier precedent:** `settings_profile\code.html` carries those values as `value=`/`<textarea>` sample data — the 0.0.180 sweep had already classified them as mockup placeholder data rather than user-facing literals. The mockup's `placeholder=` texts are the real spec and are still the form's hints; the sample values are now simply not seeded. Flagged to the user.
- **Still seeded elsewhere, deliberately out of scope:** `manage_account_screen.dart` still shows its own `Asadusjaman` / `advocate.asad@law.com` placeholders — a fresh install sees them on Manage Account too. Reported, not touched, because the request named the Profile screen only.
- **Tests (7 in `test/features/profile_name_test.dart`, rewritten from 4).** A fresh install reads a blank record and stores nothing; the record persists trimmed and survives a restart; a legacy name-only install keeps its name and loses the old key on save; the Profile screen renders no mockup sample value, shows `Your Name` and five `—`; the editor saves and reopens the whole record (`+880 1812332721` in the read view); both drawer-header tests. `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test` 630 passed, 2 skipped.
- **Version `0.0.247`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). No new dependency.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth a device check: install this build over an old one and confirm a previously saved name survives (the legacy-key migration), then confirm a clean install opens blank.

- **Scope kept to those two keys.** The alert title's `notificationJob_titleBeforeDays` takes an already-formatted `String` (the caller localizes it), and no other string on either surface carries a number.

### 2026-09-21 — Notification settings: several day offsets, and বাংলা gets the English time window — COMPLETE

- **The request (Settings > Notifications only):** day offset should allow multiple selection, so one case can alert several times; বাংলা should show the same time window English does, with the time in Bengali and AM/PM left as letters; and the window's Cancel/OK should be the size of the app's other dialog buttons.
- **The day-offset picker is now multi-select.** Flutter could not be asked for a centred footer inside its own dialog, so the path chosen with the user was to keep Flutter's time window; for the offsets the app already owns an identical control, so the View Settings multi-select dialog was **moved into `settings_controls.dart`** as `SettingsMultiSelectDialog<T>` (generic over the value type) and both screens now use it — the file whose stated purpose is stopping the Settings screens from drifting. The Notifications screen passes the offsets as `int`, `minSelected: 1` and a new `settingsNotifications_keepOneOffset` string, so the last tick cannot be removed: an empty list would be indistinguishable from the master switch being off.
- **Stored shape.** Every tick saves immediately (like the ON/OFF pair above it — the dialog has no confirm button and the header's close button just dismisses it) into `notificationDefaultOffsets`, which is the field the daily job already reads, and `notificationDayOffset` follows as the **earliest** offset ticked, so the Add/Edit Case form's default stays meaningful. The picker row shows every ticked offset in picker order; `SettingsPickerRow` now ellipsizes its one line instead of wrapping inside a 48 px row.
- **বাংলা time window.** Flutter's Bengali Material bundle asks for a 24-hour clock (`TimeOfDayFormat.H_colon_mm`), which is why the Bengali picker dropped the AM/PM toggle and put 0–23 on the dial. New `bengali_time_picker_localizations.dart` subclasses `MaterialLocalizationBn` (the framework documents subclassing for exactly this) with only `timeOfDayFormatRaw` switched to `h_colon_mm_space_a` and Bengali `DateFormat`/`NumberFormat` instances, and is applied with `Localizations.override` **around the picker alone** — `Localizations` loads the first delegate that supports the locale, so it wins without touching any other Material widget. Digits therefore read `০৮:৩০`, and AM/PM stay the Latin letters Flutter's Bengali bundle already uses (the ARB row has always shown `০৮:৩০ AM`).
- **Cancel/OK.** Flutter sizes them to their own label, which drifts with the language; a scoped `TimePickerTheme` now gives both the app's dialog action size — 112x48 at 16 pt, the same pair `confirm_dialogs.dart`, `backup_dialogs.dart` and the Exit App window use. They stay where Flutter places them (bottom-right, next to the keyboard-entry toggle): centring them needs the app to own the window, which the user declined this time.
- **Tests (2 new, 3 rewritten).** The multi-select path (tick, add, untick, the row showing `The Day Before, Before 30 Days`), the last-tick guard with its snackbar, both long-range offsets, the alarm re-arming after a tick, and a বাংলা test that opens the window, asserts AM/PM and `৩০` are on screen, and measures both action buttons at 48 high and at least 112 wide. `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test` 622 passed, 2 skipped.
- **Not mine, left in place:** the working tree also carried two Bengali spelling fixes (`settingsScreen_manageAccount`, `settingsManageAccount_title` now read `অ্যাকাউন্ট`), which `flutter gen-l10n` picked up and regenerated. They are unrelated to this change and were not touched.
- **Version `0.0.243`** (recorded by `tool/record_version.py`). No new dependency.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — run `docs/part_a_manual_test_pass.md` on the device before Task 16. Worth a device run: the Bengali time window is the first thing in the app that overrides Flutter's Material localizations, and the dial is the one part of it no widget test can look at.

### 2026-09-20 — The casing rule is Title Case, and the ALL-CAPS section headings are gone — COMPLETE

- **The correction:** an earlier pass (v0.0.242) converted Settings copy to sentence case on the misreading that the rule was "first letter capital, the rest lowercase". The intended rule is **Title Case** — the first letter of every word capital, the rest lowercase. Its uncommitted changes were discarded and re-applied the right way.
- **What was undone and what was kept.** Every ARB label the sentence-case pass had rewritten was restored to its Title Case wording (EN and generated localizations together, since the ARB is the only place casing is decided). What was kept is the one part of that pass that matches the new rule: the six `toUpperCase()` calls that turned section labels and headings into ALL CAPS are gone for good, so `Backup & Restore`, `Automatic Backup`, `Backup Status`, `Default Notification Status`, `Key Features` and the developer card's `Advocate` role label now render in Title Case rather than shouting.
- **Hard rule 11 was rewritten** in `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`: text is Title Case in the ARB itself, section headings follow the same rule, there is no render-time `toUpperCase()`/ALL-CAPS styling, proper nouns and acronyms keep their capitals, and Bengali is unaffected.
- **Tests followed the casing, not the reverse.** The assertions that matched `.toUpperCase()` (Support's note titles and channel label, About's Data Safety and Key Features, Guidelines' section labels, the Shared `SettingsSectionLabel`) and the literal `'BACKUP & RESTORE'`-style strings became their Title Case values. One real fix: View Settings' single-line-title test now scopes its measured `Text` to `SettingsPickerDialogHeader`, because the section label behind it shares the same words — the same collision the discarded pass had hit.
- **Note the mockups disagree on section headings.** `mockup_html_screens/settings_backupRestore/code.html` shows `BACKUP & RESTORE` in caps while its body labels are Title Case; hard rule 9 (mockups are mandatory) therefore conflicts with rule 11 here. Followed the user's explicit choice: Title Case everywhere, including headings.
- **Left as they are, deliberately:** a small number of values are sentences rather than labels (e.g. `paywall_limitBody`, `settingsSupport_warningBody`) where Title Case would read badly, and mockup-pinned body labels such as `Restore from backup` and `Backup to Google Drive` keep their authored casing. Bengali ARB values are untouched.
