### 2026-09-19 — Compact card view: the same pinned, foldable court headings — COMPLETE

- **What changed (user-requested scope: the compact/card view of Overdue Dates, Daily Cause List and Upcoming Cases).** The compact card list now heads each court with the same pinned, foldable `CaseTableGroup` the table uses. The court heading holds the top of the list for as long as that court's own cards are on screen and is pushed out as the next court's heading arrives; tapping the band (the whole band is the tap target) folds that court's cards away and tapping it again brings them back; a folded court is the only one showing the down chevron, because an expanded court's cards already say they are showing; and every court opens expanded. `CaseTableGroup` already took its cases as plain widgets, so the table hands it rows and the card list hands it `_buildCard`s — the pinning and fold behaviour is one implementation, not a second one for cards.
- **The fold choice is one state, read by both views.** No new setting and no ARB key: the card list reads and writes the same per-screen `case_table_collapsed_courts` entry the table does (`watchCaseTableCollapsedCourts` / `setCaseTableCourtCollapsed`, through a small per-screen `toggleCourt` helper each screen now shares between its two bodies), so a court folded in the card list opens folded in the table and the choice survives a restart and a view switch. One screen's fold still never touches another screen's copy of the same court, exactly as before.
- **What deliberately did not change.** The compact body is now a `CustomScrollView` of `CaseTableGroup`s (cards as the group's cases) instead of one `ListView` item per court, so the heading can be a pinned sliver. The cards keep the same gutters and spacing — the group's cases sliver insets by `AppSpacing.md` and spaces cases by `AppSpacing.md`, and the list is topped and tailed by `AppSpacing.md`, which is what the old `padding: EdgeInsets.all(AppSpacing.md)` did — Overdue Dates and Upcoming Cases keep their `RefreshIndicator`, and Daily Cause List's card list keeps the no-pull-to-refresh body it has always had. Upcoming's date heading stays an ordinary (non-pinned) row above each day's groups; it is now built by one `dateHeadingSliver` helper both of that screen's bodies share, so the two views cannot drift apart.
- **Tests.** `case_list_view_mode_test.dart` is 26 — the pinning test now asserts the compact list draws the same pinned groups over its cards (and no chevron while every court is expanded), a new case pins that a court's heading holds the top of the card list and hands it over to the next court after scrolling on, and another pins that tapping a heading in the card list hides that court's cards only, stores the choice for the screen, keeps it across a restart, shows it in the table as one shared fold state, and clears it when the court is re-opened. Folding in one view and asserting the other, or drawing the compact list with plain headings, fails its matching test. `flutter analyze` clean; **352 passed, 2 skipped**. Version `0.0.210`.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — Task 13 (notification scheduling + the OEM battery/auto-start setup guide the Notifications screen points at).

### 2026-09-19 — Extended view: Overdue Dates' Step/Result column back, and foldable court headings — COMPLETE

- **What changed (user-requested scope: the extended/table view of Overdue Dates, Daily Cause List and Upcoming Cases).** (1) **Overdue Dates has its own Step/Result column again**, directly before Note and identical to the other five screens': the folded "Previous Date & Step" cell of 0.0.206 is gone, the date column is now the previous date alone (red, one line, labelled from `caseCard_previousDate`), and `CaseTableRow.dateShowsStep` plus the `subStyle` hook that backed it were removed with their last users — so the step is never printed twice and an AD/HCD result fills its own Step/Result cell here like everywhere else. (2) **The court headings fold.** Tapping a heading hides that court's rows, tapping it again shows them, and the band carries a chevron pointing **down** while the cases are hidden and up while they are showing. **A court opens folded**, so the extended view greets the user with the court headings and they open only the courts they are reading; the fold state is per group inside `CaseTableGroup` (a folded group contributes its heading sliver alone, so the next court's heading comes straight up behind it) and the **whole band** is the tap target, not just the arrow. One deliberate placement call: the chevron sits at the end of the band beside the hearing count rather than under the heading, because a band tall enough for a second line would have to grow `caseTableGroupHeaderHeight` and with it every pinned-band measurement.
- **Tests.** `case_list_view_mode_test.dart` is 23 — a new test pins the folded default (two groups, no `CaseTableRow`, both bands showing the down chevron), that tapping one heading shows that court's rows only (its chevron turned over, the other still folded) and that tapping it again hides them; the Overdue tests now pin the Step/Result column beside Note, the single-line previous-date cell and the AD/HCD result filling its own Step/Result cell. Flipping the folded default, turning the chevron round, or dropping the step column from Overdue Dates each fails its matching test. `flutter analyze` clean; **349 passed, 2 skipped**. Version `0.0.208`.
- **Two harness gotchas worth recording.** (a) Because the table is laid out at its own minimum width inside a horizontal scroll view, `tester.tap` / `tester.drag` on a wide widget (`CaseTableGroup`, the `CustomScrollView`) can derive a centre outside the 800 px test viewport and **silently miss** — a warning, not a failure, so the assertion fails for the wrong reason. Taps now use `tapAt(getTopLeft(band) + (24, 8))` and the pinned-court test drags with `dragFrom` from a point inside the viewport; that drag passed before only because Overdue Dates' table was ~190 px narrower before the Step/Result column came back. (b) A `flutter test` run crashed with `PathAccessException ... sqlite3mc.dll` and left an orphan `flutter_tester.exe` holding the DLL, so every later run crashed the same way: kill that process (or clear `build/native_assets`) before retrying.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — Task 13 (notification scheduling + the OEM battery/auto-start setup guide the Notifications screen points at).

### 2026-09-19 — Extended view: the Step/Result column and pinned court headings — COMPLETE

- **What changed (user-requested scope: the extended/table view only).** (1) The table's **Step column is now "Step/Result"** and it **carries an AD/HCD case's free-text Result**. Higher courts have no step dropdown, so the result is the only value in that slot; the row shows it in the step's place, or stacked beneath the step through the same `subText` slot when a case somehow holds both. The label is composed from `caseCard_lastStep` + `addEditCaseSheet_result`, so no ARB key was added. (2) **Overdue Dates' folded cell falls back to the result** when the case has no step, so an overdue higher-court case still shows its result in the table. (3) **The court heading on the three grouped screens (Overdue Dates, Daily Cause List, Upcoming Cases) now pins to the top of the list** for as long as that court's cases are on screen, and is pushed out as the next court's heading arrives. The extended body on those screens is a `CustomScrollView` of the new `CaseTableGroup` (heading + rows in one `SliverMainAxisGroup`, the heading a pinned `SliverPersistentHeader`); the pinned band paints the screen background so rows slide underneath, and Upcoming's date heading stays an ordinary row above each day's groups. The compact card list on all three screens is untouched and keeps its scroll-away headings.
- **The grouping is load-bearing, not decoration.** Plain consecutive pinned headers in one scroll view do **not** push each other out: the viewport lays each one out at `SliverConstraints.overlap` = the pinned extent of the ones before it, so every heading ends up stacked at the top and all of them stay visible at once (measured: the first court's heading still at the viewport top with the second's 40 px below it after scrolling to the bottom). Wrapping each heading and its rows in a `SliverMainAxisGroup` scopes the pinning to the group, which is what produces the handover the user asked for.
- **One dead end worth recording.** The first attempt kept the rows in one `ListView` and moved the pinned headings into slivers only, which is why the stacking behaviour was discovered by measurement rather than assumed. `caseTableGroupHeaderHeight` sizes the pinned band from the heading's own line height (title `fontSize` × `height`), scaled with the user's text size.
- **A test-data trap worth recording.** `result` doubles as the disposal marker: a result whose text contains *Dismissed*, *Disposed* or *Discharged* (`disposedResultTermLabels`) makes `CasesDao._isDisposed` true, so the case leaves Overdue Dates / Active and appears under Disposed. The first version of the new Overdue test seeded `'Appeal dismissed'` and saw the empty state; an active AD/HCD case now seeds `'Judgment reserved'`.
- **Tests.** `case_list_view_mode_test.dart` is 22 — the header reads "Step/Result" with the bare "Step" gone, an AD/HCD result fills the step cell and a case holding both stacks the result under the step, Overdue's folded cell carries the result, the first court's heading holds the list top while its cases are visible and the second takes over after scrolling on, the pinning exists only in the extended view, and the compact lists still draw plain headings. Removing the result from the step cell, or `pinned: true` from the group, fails its matching test. `flutter analyze` clean; **348 passed, 2 skipped**. Version `0.0.207`.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — Task 13 (notification scheduling + the OEM battery/auto-start setup guide the Notifications screen points at).

### 2026-09-18 — Extended view rebuilt as an Excel-like table with resizable columns — COMPLETE

- **What changed (user-requested scope: the extended/table view only).** The extended view is now a spreadsheet instead of a squeezed card: one **header row** (`CaseTableHeader`) names every column once — **Serial → Update → Name of Court → Case Type → Case Number → Petitioner → Respondent → On Behalf → Step → Note → Date** — and each case row carries **values only** (no per-cell labels), each in a **single line** (`maxLines: 1`, `softWrap: false`, ellipsis; widen the column to read more). Rows keep the card's chrome (white surface, `cardBorder` outline, right-rounded `cardRadius`, red 4 px strip on overdue rows). The grouped screens still show their date/court sub-headings, now *below* the header row, and their court column repeats the court name.
- **Columns are resizable and the sizes persist.** Dragging a rule in the header resizes the column on its left; on drag end the whole width map is stored per user as `case_table_column_widths` inside `view_settings_json` (no schema change, no new ARB key beyond `caseTable_serial` / `caseTable_disposed`), through the same read-modify-write + `settingsViewModelProvider` invalidation the display mode uses — so nothing else in the settings row can be lost. A missing/partial/corrupt map falls back to the defaults with every value clamped to its column's 48–96 px floor and 640 px ceiling.
- **`CaseTableView` (`features/cases/presentation/case_table_view.dart`) owns the widths and the scroll.** It lays the header and the body out at one computed minimum width (`caseTableMinimumWidth`: columns + rules + the fixed chrome of a 2 px border, a 4 px strip lane, a 12 px gutter and the 16 px screen margin) inside one horizontal scroll view, so the header and the rows scroll together and the header's cells sit **exactly** over the data columns. That alignment is why the header and every row reserve the 4 px strip lane — the header never paints it, only overdue rows do. A test asserts the alignment column by column.
- **Decisions from the user, applied.** The date is the eleventh column (after Note) and is the screen's own date, named by the screen: "Next Date" on All/Active/Upcoming/Daily Cause List, "Previous Date" on Overdue Dates (the one explicit rename asked for), "Disposed" on Disposed Cases. The compact card's **phone and message actions are dropped** from the table (user's choice) — they stay on the card and the case detail screen. Fields without a column ride along in the nearest one: judges' name → Name of Court, free-text case title/section → Case Type, joined with `·`. Because the header gives every screen a Serial column, the grouped screens now number their rows too (in render order); the serial is still a position, not an identity.
- **Tests.** `case_list_view_mode_test.dart` is 13 — header/column order and header-over-cell alignment, single-line cells (including a long user-added on-behalf role and a long note), the resize drag storing its size and a later visit opening at it, a clamped width plus an unknown column id, serials and the date header on the grouped screens, no phone/message icons, and the 360 dp narrow-phone scroll. `repositories_test.dart` round-trips `case_table_column_widths` through the stored JSON. `flutter analyze` clean; **338 passed, 2 skipped**. Version `0.0.204`.
- **Note for the next test on these screens:** they hold live Drift streams, so unmount the tree (pump a bare `SizedBox`) before the test ends and prefer bounded pumps over `pumpAndSettle` — the same gotcha as the search suites.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — Task 13 (notification scheduling + the OEM battery/auto-start setup guide the Notifications screen points at).

### 2026-09-18 — Case lists: the view toggle works and the extended view exists — COMPLETE

- **What was wrong.** `CasesScreenModal` drew a compact ↔ expanded view-toggle icon in the header of **all nine** screens that use it, but no screen ever passed `onViewToggle`, so the button was enabled, tappable and did nothing (on Notes and Case Reference too, where a case-view toggle makes no sense). `case_list_display_mode` — Settings → View Settings → **Case List Display** — was stored and shown but read by nobody.
- **What it does now.** The shell draws the toggle only when its host wires it, so the two libraries lost it automatically with no extra flag. The six case-list screens read the stored mode from the new shared `core/providers/settings_providers.dart` (`userSettingsStreamProvider`) and write it through `case_list_view_mode.dart` (`setCaseListViewMode`); extended mode swaps each screen's `CaseCard` for the new shared `CaseTableRow`, and `CaseTableBody` gives the body the mockup's 600 px minimum width with a horizontal scroll. `CaseTableRow` carries **full data parity** with the card — serial cell, bordered Update column, date and step/result columns, the case block (number, parties, section, court · judges, note) and the phone/message actions — inside the mockup's row chrome (white row, outline border, card radius, red strip on overdue rows).
- **Decisions (user-approved).** Serial numbers are the screen's own running order (`index + 1` of what is visible, so a filter or the Newest-First toggle renumbers from 1) and appear **only** on the flat lists — All Cases, Active Cases, Disposed Cases — rendered through `AppDateUtils.formatDigits`, so বাংলা reads ১. The grouped screens (Daily Cause List, Upcoming Cases, Overdue Dates) keep their headings and carry no serial. Rows carry the card's full field set rather than the mockup's thinner row, and the app keeps its single cycling toggle icon instead of the mockup's two-icon pair.
- **The write path, and why.** The header writes through `settingsViewModelProvider.notifier.updateSettings` semantics via `settingsRepositoryProvider` (reading the whole row first, so no other preference can be dropped while the ViewModel state is loading) and then invalidates the ViewModel, because View Settings reads the **ViewModel**, not the stream. Without that invalidation it would keep showing the mode the app started with — the same stale-arrangement class of bug as the bottom-nav order (0.0.200). Renders read the stream, never the ViewModel, so a toggle cannot flash the compact list mid-write.
- **Tests:** new `test/features/case_list_view_mode_test.dart` (9) — toggle switches cards ↔ rows and stores the choice, a stored `expanded` opens a screen already extended, the write is visible to the settings ViewModel, flat lists number their rows (`1`, `2` / `১`, `২`), the grouped lists have no serial and overdue rows keep their strip, rows keep every card field and action, and Notes/Case Reference never show a toggle. Reverting the invalidation, the toggle gate, or the "no serial on grouped screens" rule each fails its matching test. `flutter analyze` clean; 333 passed, 2 skipped. Version `0.0.203`.
- **One real bug the tests caught (and the fix).** The new 360 dp narrow-phone test found the row overflowing by 20 px: the on-behalf chip in the case block is laid out at its intrinsic width, so a long role name — very reachable, since users add their own on-behalf names in Edit Court/Steps — pushed the case number out of the row. The chip is now width-capped at 104 px and ellipsises (the case number is the primary label), and the three column rules moved to the 8 px gutter to give the case block its width back. With the cap removed the test fails by 196 px, so the guard is real. Checking compact mode at the same width first proved the overflow was mine and not pre-existing.
- **Same gotcha as last time:** these screens hold live Drift streams (settings, plus their own queries), so any suite on them must unmount the tree before the test ends and should prefer bounded pumps over `pumpAndSettle`.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — Task 13 (notification scheduling + the OEM battery/auto-start setup guide the Notifications screen points at).

### 2026-09-18 — Notes & Case Reference: the search box is the case-list search box — COMPLETE

- **What was wrong.** Both libraries already used the shared `ScopedSearchBar`, but they rendered it as the *first row of their scrolling list* instead of letting `CasesScreenModal` mount it. So it scrolled away with the cards, sat inside the list's own 16 px padding (double-spaced), and — worse — an empty library threw it away: the empty-state branch returned a `ListView` that discarded the children list holding the bar, so an empty Notes or Case Reference library could not be searched at all.
- **The fix.** The two screens pass `isSearching`, `searchController` and `onSearchChanged` to `CasesScreenModal`, exactly as the six case-list screens do, and their bodies (`_NotesList`, `_ReferenceList`) lost the controller, the search callback and the `onCloseSearch` argument they no longer need. The bar now sits under the header, above the list, stays while the list scrolls and while the library is empty, and the header icon, the always-on (x) and tap-outside-to-hide-the-keyboard all keep working because they are the same widget and the same modal wiring as the case screens. No ARB change.
- **Tests:** new `test/features/library_search_test.dart` (3) — a loop over both libraries asserts the bar opens above the list (explicitly not a descendant of the `ListView`), closes with the (x), and does all of that with an empty library; each library's query filters only its own rows, and closing clears the query. Dropping `isSearching` from the modal call fails the placement test. `flutter analyze` clean; 324 passed, 2 skipped. Version `0.0.202`.
- **Note for future tests on these screens:** they hold live Drift streams, so a suite must unmount the widget tree (pump a bare `SizedBox`) before the test ends — disposing the provider container with a query stream live leaves a pending timer that fails the test.
- **Resume point:** unchanged — Task 13 (notification scheduling + the OEM battery/auto-start setup guide the Notifications screen points at).

### 2026-09-18 — View Settings: the Bottom Navigation order is remembered — COMPLETE

- **What was wrong.** The dropdown re-seeded its rows from the canonical `bottomNavTabDefs` order every time it opened. A drag did take effect in the cases screen — it rewrote `bottom_nav_tabs`, which the bar renders — but the dropdown showed the original list again, so the reorder looked forgotten.
- **The fix.** The row order is now the user's own per-user value, `bottom_nav_order` (inside `view_settings_json`, no schema change), written by the same drag that rewrites the tab order. The dropdown therefore opens in exactly the order the user left, and the bar and the dropdown cannot disagree. An empty or partial serial falls back to the canonical order for the screens it does not name, so an install that never reordered a row is unchanged and a screen added later still appears (at its canonical place).

### 2026-09-17 — Case list search: the header icon works on all six case screens — COMPLETE

- **What was wrong.** Every case screen passed `onSearch: () {}` to `CasesScreenModal`, so the header search icon was inert: tapping it did nothing at all, on All Cases, Active, Upcoming, Daily Cause List, Overdue and Disposed. (The icon itself, the tooltip and the `searchQuery` parameter were all scaffolded in Task 06; only the wiring was missing. All Cases even kept an unused `_searchController` from the search box Task 08 removed.)
- **The bar.** `CasesScreenModal` gained `isSearching` / `searchController` / `onSearchChanged` and now renders a prompt search bar between the header and the body (prefix search icon, clear button once there is text). The **host screen keeps the state and the query**, matching the Notes / Case Reference pattern, so each list filters *its own* dataset; `onSearch` is the toggle (the header icon closes the bar and clears the query).
- **One matcher, six lists.** New `lib/features/cases/presentation/case_search.dart` with `matchesCaseQuery(case, query, {onBehalfName})` — case-insensitive substring over case number, petitioner, respondent, both lead counsels, party phone, case title/section and remarks (the field set `CasesDao.search` uses) plus the represented client name from the case's on-behalf row. Plan §12.1's rule is met literally: each screen applies its **own** filter first (court · chronology, non-disposed, upcoming window, selected date, overdue range, disposed set) and the text query second, so “stale” filters the Overdue list and “karim” the Active one. All six screens got `_isSearching` + `_toggleSearch` + a controller (disposed on unmount), and the query is threaded through the existing group/date builders (`_dateGroups`, `_courtGroups`, `_visibleCases`).
- **Empty state.** New `CaseListEmptyState`: while a scoped search hides every row the list reads “No results found.” (`sharedDialogs_noResults`, already in both ARB files) instead of showing the screen's own empty title — no ARB change was needed.
- **Tests:** new `test/features/case_list_search_test.dart` (7): the matcher's field set, case-insensitivity, the on-behalf name and null-safe misses; the All Cases flow (icon opens the bar, a query filters, the clear button empties it, the icon closes it); the no-results state; and a six-screen loop asserting the matching case stays and a case of that screen's dataset goes, each with a pre-search guard so a “hidden” assertion cannot pass because the card was merely off-screen. Mutations confirm the guards: restoring the inert `onPressed: () {}` fails all three widget tests, and dropping the query from the All Cases filter fails the flow test.
- `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test` **310 passed, 2 skipped**. Version `0.0.195`.
- **Flagged (deliberate deviation):** this is Task 14 Phase 5 work pulled forward at the user's request, and it filters the rows the screen already loaded instead of running the Drift `LIKE` queries that task specifies. `CasesDao.search` / `CaseRepository.search` therefore stay unused; the in-memory matcher is functionally equivalent (the loaded rows are the superset and carry every searchable field) and instant, but if Task 14 wants the DAO path it can swap `matchesCaseQuery` for `repository.search` without touching the UI. Also unchanged: Notes and Case Reference keep their own inline body fields (they already worked); `CasesScreenModal.searchQuery` is still an unused pass-through.

### 2026-09-17 — Upcoming Cases: Bengali date title spells the month out + dynamic divider — COMPLETE

- **Bengali got the full month back, English did not move.** `_dateGroups` no longer formats inline: the new `_formatDateTitle(dayStart, locale)` picks `EEE, dd MMMM, yyyy` under বাংলা (`বৃহস্পতি, ২৪ সেপ্টেম্বর, ২০২৬` — the language's own day-first order) and keeps `EEE, MMM dd, yyyy` for English (`THU, SEP 19, 2026`), so the English title is byte-for-byte what 0.0.193 shipped. The header still upper-cases the result, which is a no-op for Bengali.

The title is now a plain `Text` (`maxLines: 1`, `softWrap: false`, `overflow: ellipsis` as a narrow-screen guard) that lays itself out at its own width, with only the divider flexing — so the rule fills the space left over once the date is drawn. A 4 px `SizedBox` (`AppSpacing.xs`) separates them.

- **Tests:** `test/features/upcoming_cases_test.dart` is 8. The Bengali test now requires the `EEE, dd MMMM, yyyy` title and asserts the abbreviated `EEE, MMM dd, yyyy` one is absent; the English test still requires the short pattern; the new “the divider takes only the space left after the date” pumps a 500 px surface and requires the date on a single line with the divider starting right after it. Both new tests fail against the old code — with the short Bengali pattern the title is not found, and with the two-`Expanded` row the title measures 32 px (two lines) — so they are real guards. `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test` **303 passed, 2 skipped**. Case Detail, the drawer and the court-list screens already went through the seed lookup.
- **Tests:** `daily_cause_list_test.dart` gained “the court title follows the active language” and `task07_acceptance_test.dart` gained “a seeded court name on the card follows the active language” — each inserts the seeded court category and a real pre-seeded court (English name, as the seed migration writes it), one case, then pumps under `Locale('bn')` and requires the Bengali label and no English one. ### 2026-09-17 — Settings → Language redesign — COMPLETE

- **The screen no longer reads as empty.** `language_screen.dart` was a `Padding` + `Row` of two 48 px buttons, so the page ended ~48 px into an ~800 px body. It is now one group centred in the viewport: a 72 px round language badge (`Icons.language` on `surfaceContainer`), a `settingsLanguage_selectHeading` heading with a one-line `settingsLanguage_intro` under it, the two option cards, a preview card showing **today's date in the active language**, and a `settingsLanguage_note` card that says the change affects only the interface. Heights: badge 72 + heading + 2 × 84 px cards + preview + note ≈ 560 px, so the body is filled with content the user reads rather than with padding.
- **Layout mechanics:** `LayoutBuilder` → `SingleChildScrollView` (16 px edge padding) → `ConstrainedBox(minHeight: viewport − 32)` → `Center` → `ConstrainedBox(maxWidth: 480)` → `Column(mainAxisSize: min)`. A tall screen centres the group; a short one (320 × 480) or a large text scale scrolls instead of overflowing — the test asserts no exception at 320 × 480 with `textScaler` 1.3.
- **Options:** 84 px, full-width, radius 4. The active language is filled black with white text and a white `Icons.check`; the other is white with black text, an `AppColors.outline` border and a transparent check so the label never shifts. `Semantics(selected:, button:)` plus the check's `semanticLabel` (`settingsLanguage_selected`) carry the state to screen readers, and each label keeps its own script's font (`titleMd` / new `bengaliTitleMd`).
- **Preview is the proof:** `AppDateUtils.formatFullDate(today, locale: l.localeName)` in the active script's title font, so switching language visibly changes the line — English "Thursday, September 17, 2026" vs Bengali "বৃহস্পতিবার, সেপ্টেম্বর ১৭, ২০২৬".
- **New token:** `AppTypography.bengaliTitleMd` (Tiro Bangla 16 / 24, w600) — the Bengali counterpart of `titleMd`, symmetric with the existing `bengaliBodyLg/Md/Sm` set.
- **ARB:** new `@@_section_settingsLanguage` section (en + bn) with `settingsLanguage_selectHeading` / `_intro` / `_previewTitle` / `_selected` / `_note`; the two autonyms `settingsScreen_languageEnglish` / `_Bengali` moved into it (`settingsScreen_language` stays in the settings-main section, which renders it as the main-list row). `test/l10n/arb_keys_test.dart` gained `settingsLanguage` to the marker guard.
- **Mockup:** new `mockup_html_screens/settings_language/code.html` (the screen had none — Texts.txt lists no reference for it), showing the composition twice side by side, English active and বাংলা active. No `screen.png` yet.
- **Tests:** `language_screen_test.dart` rewritten to 8 (framing, black/outline states, a check painted only on the active option, the `selected` flag + check semantics, per-script fonts, the preview date following the locale, persistence, and the short/large-text layout). `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test` **266 passed, 2 skipped**. Version `0.0.182`.

### 2026-09-17 — Task 12: labels → ARB keys (localization sweep) — COMPLETE

- **Answer to the question that started it:** the Settings *screens* were NOT keyed. Only their titles/section names existed in `app_en.arb` / `app_bn.arb` (`settingsScreen_*`, `settings*_title`); every body label on Settings, Account & Profile, Profile, Manage Account, Online Sync, Backup & Restore and the whole Edit Court/Steps tree was a hard-coded English string in Dart, so editing the ARB could not change it. Both files did already agree with each other (256 keys, same order, no key present in only one language).
- **Now:** those labels are keys and are read through `AppLocalizations`. **137 keys added (256 → 393)**; the earlier count of hard-coded user-facing literals was ~150 across `lib/`, and all but the dev-only scratch screens (`core/scratch`, `features/scratch`) and the four `— Task 12`/`— Task 20` placeholder bodies are now keyed. Non-labels deliberately left alone: the `—` empty-value dash, the `+880` phone prefix, the `1XXXXXXXXX` phone-format hint, mockup placeholder data (`Dhaka Bar Association`, `1557`, `Chamber 402 …`, `1812332721`, Manage Account's `Asadusjaman` / `advocate.asad@law.com`) and route/DB ids.
- **Organised by screen:** both ARB files were rewritten so keys are contiguous per screen, each screen preceded by an `@@_section_<id>` marker row whose value names the section and the source file, e.g. `"@@_section_settingsBackupRestore": "SETTINGS - BACKUP & RESTORE  |  lib/features/settings/presentation/backup_restore_screen.dart"`. Flutter's ARB parser ignores `@`-prefixed keys, so the markers are invisible to `flutter gen-l10n` (verified with a real gen-l10n run) and to the repo's own `tool/generate_l10n_dart*.py` / `generate_l10n.py`, which skip them too. Consequence: the three checked-in generated files (`app_localizations*.dart`) are re-ordered because gen-l10n emits getters in ARB order — large but purely mechanical diff.
- **Section order** follows the app: APP WIDE → NAVIGATION → per case screen → ADD/EDIT CASE SHEET → CASES MODAL → CONTACT SHEET → NOTES → CASE REFERENCE → SYNC → SHARED BUTTONS/DIALOGS → APP SHELL → DATABASE RECOVERY → SEED DATA (court categories / on-behalf / case types / steps / divisions) → MESSAGE TEMPLATES → then the SETTINGS cluster (main list → Account & Profile → Profile → Manage Account → Online Sync → Backup & Restore → Edit Court/Steps → Edit Court Names & Categories → Court Names, Steps etc → Add/Edit Item → Court list → Divisions/Districts/Lower Court Categories → Notifications/View/Guidelines/About) → dev-only localization scratch.
- **Wired in code** (all labels now read from the ARB, English values unchanged so no test needed rewriting): `settings_main_screen.dart` — section + item titles are built per frame from the localizations (search now matches in the active language; new `settingsScreen_section*`, `*_searchTooltip`, `*_searchHint`, `*_closeSearchTooltip`, `*_noResults`); `accounts_profile_menu_screen.dart`, `profile_screen.dart` (data rows, edit sheet, `settingsProfile_displayName('Adv. {name}')`, save toast), `manage_account_screen.dart`, `online_sync_screen.dart` (incl. `{count} Items`), `backup_restore_screen.dart` (schedule options + section headings, now upper-cased in the widget so translations stay sentence-case), `edit_court_steps_menu.dart`, `edit_court_names_category_screen.dart`, `court_names_steps_screen.dart` (the 8 category labels), `item_list_screen.dart` (kind titles, short category labels, dialogs, empty/error states), `court_list_screen.dart`, `court_category_screen.dart`, `district_list_screen.dart` (`{division} Division`), `lower_court_category_list_screen.dart` (`{district} Court Categories`), `language_screen.dart` (reuses `settingsScreen_languageEnglish/Bengali`; **note** the English UI now shows "Bengali" rather than the autonym "বাংলা" — change the `en` value if the autonym is wanted), `app_router.dart` (exit dialog: `appShell_*`), `database_recovery_dialog.dart`, `app.dart` (`onGenerateTitle` → `appTitle`), and the six case screens (`casesContact_*`, `casesScreen_loadError`, `caseDetailsScreen_deleteCaseError`, `caseDetailsScreen_deleteHistoryError`, `caseDetailsScreen_loadError`).
- **Guard test** `test/l10n/arb_keys_test.dart` (4 tests): en/bn declare the same keys in the same order, no empty values, bn keeps every placeholder the en message uses (ICU plural/select messages skipped — a naive parser reads `=1{...}` as a placeholder), and the settings section markers exist. Also `test/features/profile_picture_test.dart` and `test/database/database_guard_test.dart` now supply `AppLocalizations.localizationsDelegates` (those screens read the ARB now; the harnesses had bare `MaterialApp`s).
- `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test` **207 passed, 2 skipped** (was 203 + 2). `dart format` applied to the touched files only; 12 files the formatter also wanted to reformat (pre-existing style drift: `settings_viewmodel.dart`, `disposed_help_dialog.dart`, `history_entry_edit_dialog.dart`, the `settings_*` barrels, …) were reverted so the diff stays scoped. Version `0.0.174`. **Resume point:** Task 12 Phase 3 (remaining Edit Court/Steps polish); the four `— Task 12` placeholder screens (Notifications, View Settings, Guidelines, About) still hold dev placeholder text and were not keyed.

- **Schema v7**: `court_categories` gained nullable `division` + `district` (`court_categories_table.dart`, migration `from < 7` in `app_database.dart`; Drift code regenerated). `court_categories_repository.dart` now reports `division`/`district` as changed fields. Custom categories are therefore **district-scoped**, exactly as the user asked; built-ins stay global (null scope).
- **Add/Edit Case form** (`shared/widgets/add_edit_case_sheet.dart`): `_lowerCategories` now calls the shared helper, so a category added in Settings shows up in the form's Court Category dropdown for that district only. Its inline filter/court-count logic was replaced by the helper.
- **Tests** `test/features/lower_court_category_test.dart` (15): derivation (hidden 0-court categories, district-scoped customs, higher-court exclusion), the **real pre-seeded hierarchy** (Dhaka → civil, sessions, metropolitan sessions, CJM, CMM, executive in that order; Gopalganj → civil, sessions, CJM, executive, i.e. the metropolitan categories are omitted — the seed has no tribunals category at all), ordering helpers, repository district scope + built-in delete refusal, screen listing/add/rename/delete/FAB parity, and the form offering the district's custom category. `flutter analyze` clean; `flutter test` 203 passed, 2 skipped. Version `0.0.173`.
- Notes for the next session: the reorder **interaction** is covered by unit tests on the slot mapping, not by an end-to-end drag test; renaming a built-in writes a literal `name_key`, so that category then shows the same text in Bengali as in English (the add/rename dialog also short-circuits when the text is unchanged, which protects built-ins from an accidental wipe). Drift-backed widget tests leave a pending stream timer at teardown, so this screen's widget tests inject in-memory fakes instead — a `tearDown` that closes a Drift database in a `testWidgets` test can deadlock. **Resume point:** Task 12 Phase 3 (remaining Edit Court/Steps polish).

Now every `InputDecoration` border state (`border`, `enabledBorder`, `focusedBorder`, `disabledBorder`, `errorBorder`, `focusedErrorBorder`) is set to `InputBorder.none` on both fields.

### 2026-09-05 — Task 10 Case History date ordering + Step alignment — COMPLETE

- Case Detail **Case History section only**: rows now sort **newest-first by each row's own displayed date** (`hearing_date`) instead of the DAO's stored `next_date` key, so the visible timeline always reads chronologically even when entries were added or edited out of date order (secondary keys: `next_date`, then `updated_at`, keep same-day rows deterministic). The DAO keeps its `next_date` contract for other callers; the screen re-sorts in `_load`.

### 2026-09-02 — Task 09 fixes (post-Phase 4, 2): calendar selection off-by-one + dialog height — COMPLETE

- Bug: selecting a date in the calendar picked the previous day (e.g. tapping 03/09/2026 selected 02/09/2026). Root cause: `AppDateUtils.bangladeshCivilDateEpoch` built the civil-day start-of-day from a **local**-midnight `DateTime(year, month, day)`, which on a UTC+6 (Bangladesh) device shifts the epoch back ~6h into the prior UTC day. Fixed to use `DateTime.utc(year, month, day)` so the cell epoch matches the labeled civil date on any device timezone. This also keeps grid cell keys consistent with `normalizeToBangladeshStartOfDay` (used by the badge counts), so badges stay correct.
- Enhancement: reduced the calendar dialog height by ~5% only (`minHeight` 0.70→0.665, `maxHeight` 0.92→0.874 of screen height). Width unchanged (360–460).

Version `0.0.115`. Known issues: none.

### 2026-09-02 — Task 09 fixes (post-Phase 4): badge calendar badges, black screen, dialog size — COMPLETE

- Bug: date tap opened a black screen. Root cause: `_DayCell.onTap` both invoked `widget.onDateSelected(epoch)` (which already pops the dialog with the value) AND called `Navigator.of(context).pop()` again, double-popping the route and sending the app back to a black state. Fixed by removing the redundant `Navigator.pop()` — the dialog closes once via the single pop in the screen's `onDateSelected` callback.
- Bug: badges never showed even on dates with cases. Root cause: `CasesDao.countByNextDateRange` grouped by the raw stored `nextDate` epoch, which only equals `bangladeshCivilDateEpoch(day)` when the device is in Bangladesh timezone. Fixed by normalizing each count key with `AppDateUtils.normalizeToBangladeshStartOfDay`, and normalizing the screen's month range bounds, so grid lookups match on any device timezone.
- Enhancement: enlarged the calendar dialog (`minHeight` 0.7 → `maxHeight` 0.92 of screen height, width 360–460) and made day cells taller (`childAspectRatio` 1 → 1.25) so the month grid is larger. The selected non-today date now uses a visible black ring (was an invisible secondary ring).

Version `0.0.114`. No remaining known issues in the Daily Cause List / badge calendar.

### 2026-08-25 — Disposed Cases: auto-reload after Quick Update — COMPLETE

- Root cause: `_openQuickUpdate` opened `QuickUpdateCaseDialog` but did not reload afterward. Reviving a case (removing disposal steps via Quick Update) left it in the Disposed list until a manual pull-to-refresh.
- Fix: added `if (mounted) await _loadCases();` after the dialog closes — matching the existing pattern in `all_cases_screen.dart`. The list now reflects the change immediately.

Version `0.0.102`.

### 2026-08-25 — Disposal detection used wrong label set — COMPLETE

- Root cause: `CasesDao` matched the Steps field against a hardcoded two-word list `['disposed', 'নিষ্পত্তি']` (`_disposedLabels`). But `last_step` stores the actual comma-joined **step names** (e.g. `"Withdrawal,Dismissal"`), so selecting disposal steps like Withdrawal/Dismissal/Allowed/Rejected never matched and the case stayed in Active/Upcoming/Overdue.
- Fix: derived `disposedStepNames` (both languages) from `pre_seeded_data.disposedStepIds` via `stepLabelEn`/`stepLabelBn`, and switched `_stepIsDisposed`/`_stepIsNotDisposed` to match against that full set with substring `LIKE`. Now any case whose Steps contain a disposal step name is correctly treated as disposed.

Version `0.0.101`.

### 2026-08-25 — Disposal detection: match label inside mixed Step/Result — COMPLETE

- Root cause: `CasesDao._isDisposed` matched `lastStep` with `isIn(_disposedLabels)` (exact equality). District-court steps are stored comma-joined (e.g. `"Disposed, Argument"`), so a disposed step mixed with others failed the exact match and the case was treated as active.
- Fix: disposal now uses `LIKE '%disposed%'` / `'%নিষ্পত্তি%'` substring matching for both the Steps field and the AD/HCD Result field. A case is disposed whenever either field *contains* a disposal label, even alongside other steps/words. The active-case count queries were updated to use the new `_stepIsNotDisposed` expression.

Version `0.0.100`.

### 2026-08-25 — Keep disposed cases out of non-disposed screens — COMPLETE

- Root cause: `CasesDao.getByNextDateRange` did not exclude disposed cases, so disposed cases leaked into Upcoming, Overdue, and Daily Cause List screens.
- Fix: added `_isDisposed.not()` to the date-range query. Disposed cases now appear only in Active (excluded) and Disposed (only-source) screens, plus the unfiltered All Cases screen — matching the intended scope.

Version `0.0.99`.
